\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Migraine and Seizures Symptoms and Signs} Migraine can cause recurrent headache with nausea, light sensitivity, or aura, while seizures result from abnormal electrical brain activity and may cause altered awareness, movements, sensations, or post-event confusion. Overlap exists, so history and neurologic evaluation are important.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Migraine and Stroke Symptoms} Migraine aura usually develops gradually and resolves, whereas stroke symptoms often begin suddenly and cause persistent focal deficits such as weakness, speech trouble, facial droop, or vision loss. New or atypical neurologic symptoms require emergency stroke assessment.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Migraine vs Headache Whats the Difference} A headache is a symptom with many causes; migraine is a neurologic disorder with recurrent attacks often involving throbbing pain, nausea, light or sound sensitivity, and sometimes aura. Diagnosis depends on pattern, associated symptoms, and examination.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{milk intolerance (Lactose Intolerance)} Alternate index label for Lactose Intolerance.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mini Stroke (Transient Ischemic Attack (TIA, Mini-Stroke))} Alternate index label for Transient Ischemic Attack (TIA, Mini-Stroke).

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mini-Stroke vs. Stroke (Stroke vs Mini-Stroke TIA Comparison)} Alternate index label for Stroke vs Mini-Stroke TIA Comparison.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Miscarriage Causes and Symptoms} Miscarriage is loss of a pregnancy before viability and may cause vaginal bleeding, cramping, passage of tissue, or no symptoms. Causes often involve chromosomal abnormalities, but evaluation considers ectopic pregnancy, infection, uterine factors, and recurrent-loss conditions.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Misophonia} Misophonia is an intense emotional or physiologic reaction to specific repetitive sounds, such as chewing or tapping. It is not simple annoyance; behavioral strategies, sound management, and therapy can reduce impairment, while assessment considers anxiety, obsessive-compulsive symptoms, and sensory sensitivity.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Moderate to Severe Disabilities} Moderate to severe disability describes substantial limitations in physical, cognitive, sensory, communication, or adaptive functioning that require ongoing support. Needs vary widely and may include medical care, rehabilitation, assistive technology, education, and assistance with daily activities.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mold Exposure} Mold exposure can irritate the eyes, skin, and airways and may worsen asthma or allergic rhinitis; invasive infection is uncommon and mainly affects severely immunocompromised people. Reducing moisture and remediating contaminated areas are more useful than unproven detoxification.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Moles} Moles are common collections of pigment-producing melanocytes and are usually benign. A lesion that changes in size, shape, color, symmetry, or symptoms, or differs markedly from others, should be examined for melanoma.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Molluscum Contagiosum Facts} Molluscum contagiosum is a contagious poxvirus infection causing small, firm, centrally indented skin bumps. It often resolves spontaneously, but treatment may be considered for discomfort, spread, eczema, genital lesions, or immune suppression.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Monilia Infection Children (Thrush and Other Yeast Infections in Children)} Alternate index label for Thrush and Other Yeast Infections in Children.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mono (Infectious Mononucleosis)} Alternate index label for Infectious Mononucleosis.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mononucleos Contagious (Is Mononucleosis (Mono) Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Mononucleosis (Mono) Contagious.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Morgellons Disease} Morgellons is a controversial condition involving distressing skin sensations and the belief that fibers or materials emerge from the skin. Symptoms are real to the person and require respectful evaluation for dermatologic, neurologic, psychiatric, toxic, or infectious causes without reinforcing an unproven explanation.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mortons Neuroma (Morton's Neuroma)} Alternate index label for Morton's Neuroma.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Motion Sickness (Sea Sickness, Car Sickness)} Alternate index label for Sea Sickness, Car Sickness.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Motor Fluctuations Causes in Parkinson’s Disease} Motor fluctuations in Parkinson disease are changes between good mobility and wearing-off or medication-related involuntary movements as dopamine treatment duration and disease progression change. Adjusting timing, formulations, adjunct medicines, infusion, or surgery may help.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MPS I (MPS I Mucopolysaccharidosis Type I Hurler Syndrome)} Alternate index label for MPS I Mucopolysaccharidosis Type I Hurler Syndrome.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MPS I Mucopolysaccharidosis Type I Hurler Syndrome} Mucopolysaccharidosis type I is an inherited lysosomal storage disease caused by alpha-L-iduronidase deficiency. Hurler syndrome is the severe form, with developmental regression, skeletal and airway disease, organ enlargement, corneal clouding, and cardiac involvement; early specialist treatment matters.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MPS II MucopolysaccharidosII Hunter Syndrome (Hunter Syndrome Mucopolysaccharidosis II MPS)} Alternate index label for Hunter Syndrome Mucopolysaccharidosis II MPS.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MRSA Contagious (Is MRSA Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is MRSA Contagious.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MRSA Infection} Methicillin-resistant Staphylococcus aureus is a strain of staphylococcus resistant to several beta-lactam antibiotics. It can colonize without illness or cause skin, wound, lung, bone, joint, or bloodstream infection; culture-guided treatment and hygiene reduce spread.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MS (Multiple Sclerosis (MS))} Alternate index label for Multiple Sclerosis (MS).

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MS (Multiple Sclerosis) Symptoms and Treatments} Alternate index label for Multiple Sclerosis) Symptoms and Treatment.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MS Alternative and Complementary Therapies (Alternative Therapy for Multiple Sclerosis)} Alternate index label for Alternative Therapy for Multiple Sclerosis.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MS Contagious (Is MS Contagious Multiple Sclerosis Transmission)} Alternate index label for Is MS Contagious Multiple Sclerosis Transmission.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MSBP (Munchausen Syndrome by Proxy)} Alternate index label for Munchausen Syndrome by Proxy.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mucinous Cyst Adenomas Pancreas (Pancreatic Cysts)} Alternate index label for Pancreatic Cysts.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{mucocutaneous lymph node syndrome (Kawasaki Disease)} Alternate index label for Kawasaki Disease.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MucopolysaccharidosII Hunter Syndrome (Hunter Syndrome Mucopolysaccharidosis II MPS)} Alternate index label for Hunter Syndrome Mucopolysaccharidosis II MPS.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Multidrug-Resistant Extensively Drug-Resistant TB} Multidrug-resistant tuberculosis is resistant to key first-line drugs, while extensively drug-resistant TB has additional resistance that greatly limits options. Diagnosis requires molecular or culture testing, and treatment uses prolonged individualized combinations with public-health oversight.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Multiple Myeloma: Deadly to Chronic} Multiple myeloma is a plasma-cell cancer that produces a monoclonal protein and can cause bone lesions, anemia, kidney injury, high calcium, infections, and fatigue. It is often treatable as a chronic illness, though relapse and complications remain possible.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Multiple Sclerosis (MS)} Alternate index label for MS.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Multiple Sclerosis MS and Pregnancy} Multiple sclerosis usually does not prevent pregnancy, but symptoms, disease-modifying medicines, relapse risk, delivery planning, and postpartum care require coordination. Some treatments must be stopped or changed, and relapse risk can shift after delivery.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Multiple Sclerosis MS Early Signs and Types} Multiple sclerosis is an immune-mediated disease of the central nervous system with episodes or progression of visual, sensory, motor, balance, bladder, cognitive, or fatigue symptoms. Relapsing, progressive, and other patterns guide testing and treatment.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Murmur Congenital (Heart Murmur)} Alternate index label for Heart Murmur.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Murmur Heart (Heart Murmur)} Alternate index label for Heart Murmur.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Muscle Spasms} Muscle spasms are sudden involuntary contractions caused by exertion, dehydration, electrolyte disturbance, nerve disease, medicines, pregnancy, or musculoskeletal problems. Recurrent, widespread, painful, or weakness-associated spasms need evaluation for an underlying cause.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Muscular Dystrophy: Symptoms, Causes and Types} Muscular dystrophies are inherited disorders causing progressive muscle fiber weakness and degeneration. Different types vary in age of onset, inheritance, heart and breathing involvement, contractures, and progression; genetic diagnosis, rehabilitation, and cardiac and respiratory monitoring are important.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Myelodysplastic or Myeloproliferative Neoplasms} Myelodysplastic and myeloproliferative neoplasms are clonal bone-marrow disorders causing abnormal blood-cell production. They may produce anemia, infection, bleeding, high counts, enlarged spleen, thrombosis, or progression to acute leukemia; classification and mutation testing guide care.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Myelodysplastic Syndromes (MDS)} Alternate index label for MDS.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{myocardial infarction (Heart Attack)} Alternate index label for Heart Attack.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MAB} Abbreviation for monoclonal antibody; the suffix \textit{-mab} in a generic drug name indicates a monoclonal antibody, such as adalimumab or rituximab.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Macerate} To soften tissue by soaking and enzymatic digestion, as may occur in a stillborn fetus after death.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Macewen sign} An unusually resonant sound produced by percussion of the skull near the junction of the frontal, temporal, and parietal bones, classically associated with hydrocephalus or a brain abscess.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Machine, CPM} A continuous passive motion machine that repeatedly moves a limb, such as a surgically treated knee, through a prescribed range of motion while the patient relaxes.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Machupo virus} An arenavirus that causes Bolivian hemorrhagic fever, a potentially fatal illness characterized by fever, pain, and bleeding; it is endemic in parts of Bolivia.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Macro- (prefix)} A prefix meaning large, abnormally large, or on a large scale, as in macrocytic, meaning involving abnormally large cells.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Macroangiopathy} Disease of large blood vessels, often involving atherosclerotic buildup or thrombosis that narrows or blocks blood flow; examples include coronary, cerebrovascular, and peripheral vascular disease.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Macrobiotic} Relating to the macrobiota, the living organisms in a region that are large enough to be seen with the naked eye.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Macrocytic} Describing a cell, especially a red blood cell, that is abnormally large; macrocytic anemia features enlarged red blood cells.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Macrogenitosomia} Premature or abnormal enlargement of the external genitalia, usually associated with a hormonal disorder that may also affect the internal sex organs.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Macrognathia} Abnormal enlargement of the jaw, which may be associated with pituitary gigantism, tumors, or other disorders; also called a prognathic mandible.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Macroscopic} Large enough to be seen with the naked eye, without a microscope; the opposite of microscopic.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Macular (skin)} Relating to a macule, a small, flat, circumscribed change in skin color that is neither raised nor depressed.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Macular cyst} A cystic defect in the macula, the central retinal area responsible for sharp vision, often associated with vitreous traction and potentially causing distortion or loss of central vision.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Macules} Small, flat, circumscribed changes in skin color that are neither raised nor depressed; they are distinguished from raised papules and fluid-filled vesicles.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Magic bullet} An idealized treatment that selectively cures a disease with little or no harm to the patient; the term was introduced by Paul Ehrlich in connection with targeted treatment of infection.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Magnet therapy} An alternative therapy that places magnets on or near the body to relieve pain or treat disease. Scientific evidence does not support the claimed therapeutic benefits.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Major} Larger, greater, or more significant than another related structure or condition; for example, the teres major muscle is larger than the teres minor muscle.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Malacia} Abnormal softening of a tissue or organ, often caused by degeneration or inadequate structural support; examples include bronchomalacia and osteomalacia.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Male genitalia} The external and internal reproductive organs of a male, including the penis, scrotum, testes, epididymides, vas deferens, seminal vesicles, prostate, and associated ducts and glands.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Male pelvis} The lower part of the abdomen between the hip bones in a male; compared with the female pelvis, it is generally narrower, taller, and more robust, with a narrower pubic arch and sacrum.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Malformation} A structural defect caused by abnormal development of an embryo or fetus; examples include cleft lip and cleft palate.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Manic-depression} An older term for bipolar disorder, a mood disorder characterized by episodes of mania or hypomania and episodes of depression.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Manorexia} An informal, nonmedical term sometimes used for anorexia nervosa in males; it is not an officially recognized diagnostic term.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MAOI} Abbreviation for monoamine oxidase inhibitor, a drug that inhibits monoamine oxidase and is used in selected treatment of depression and other disorders.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MAP} Common abbreviation for \textit{Mycobacterium avium} subspecies \textit{paratuberculosis}, an organism of the \textit{M. avium} complex; the abbreviation can have other meanings in different clinical contexts.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Map-dot-fingerprint type corneal dystrophy} A usually bilateral corneal epithelial basement membrane dystrophy producing map-like, dot-like, or fingerprint-like patterns and sometimes recurrent corneal erosions or visual blurring.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mapping} Charting the location of genes or other identifiable regions on chromosomes; the term may also describe charting functional or anatomical regions in other medical contexts.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Marie-Strumpell disease} Ankylosing spondylitis, a chronic inflammatory disease primarily affecting the sacroiliac joints and spine that can cause back pain, stiffness, and progressive spinal fusion.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Marker} An identifiable biological feature used to track a gene, cell, disease, or process; a genetic marker is a detectable DNA region inherited with a nearby gene, while a blood or tumor marker is a measurable substance associated with a condition.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Marker, blood} A measurable sign of a disease or condition that can be isolated or detected in a blood sample, such as a disease-associated antibody, hormone, protein, or other substance.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MASA syndrome} An X-linked neurodevelopmental disorder characterized by intellectual disability, absent or limited speech, shuffling gait, spasticity, and adducted or clasped thumbs; it may also include short stature and lumbar lordosis. The name is an older acronym and terminology should be used sensitively.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Masklike face} An expressionless face with little spontaneous animation, also called masklike facies; it may occur in Parkinson disease, myotonic dystrophy, and other disorders.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Materia medica} The branch of medical science concerned with drugs used to treat disease, including their pharmacology, clinical use, history, and physical and chemical properties; the term can also mean the drugs themselves.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Maternal serum alpha-fetoprotein (MSAFP)} A blood test measuring alpha-fetoprotein in a pregnant person's serum, usually during the second trimester, used as a screening component for fetal conditions such as open neural-tube defects and certain chromosomal abnormalities; an abnormal result requires appropriate follow-up rather than serving as a diagnosis by itself.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Materteral} Relating to a maternal aunt or to the genetic relationship between an aunt and her niece or nephew; synonymous with materterine.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MB ChB} Abbreviation for Bachelor of Medicine and Bachelor of Surgery, a medical degree awarded in many countries.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{McBurney's point} A surface point in the right lower abdomen, classically near the junction of the lateral and middle thirds of a line from the anterior superior iliac spine to the umbilicus, where maximal tenderness may occur in acute appendicitis.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MCD} An abbreviation with several medical meanings, including minimal change disease, mean corpuscular diameter, and medullary cystic disease; the intended meaning depends on context.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MCH} Mean corpuscular hemoglobin: the calculated average amount of hemoglobin in each red blood cell, reported as part of a complete blood count.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MCHC} Mean corpuscular hemoglobin concentration: the calculated average concentration of hemoglobin in a given volume of packed red blood cells, derived from hemoglobin and hematocrit values and reported in a complete blood count.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MCP} Membrane cofactor protein, also called CD46, is a cell-surface protein that regulates complement activation and helps protect host cells from complement-mediated injury; the abbreviation can have other meanings in different contexts.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MCV} Mean corpuscular volume: the calculated average volume of a red blood cell, derived from hematocrit and red-cell count and reported as part of a complete blood count.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MDI} An abbreviation that may mean metered-dose inhaler, multiple daily injections, or mental developmental index; the intended meaning depends on context.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MDMA} 3,4-Methylenedioxymethamphetamine, a psychoactive stimulant and hallucinogen commonly known as ecstasy or molly; it can produce serious cardiovascular, neurologic, and temperature-regulation effects.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Me-too drug} Me-too drug is a medicine, biologic, or pharmacologic term; its use, dose, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific clinical indication.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mean cell volume} The average volume of a red blood cell, calculated from the hematocrit and red-cell count and reported as MCV in a complete blood count.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Meatus, female urethral} The external opening of the female urethra, through which urine leaves the body; it is located anterior and superior to the vaginal opening.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Medical entomology} The study of insects and related arthropods that cause disease or transmit disease-causing organisms to humans, including their biology, ecology, physiology, and genetics.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Medical etymology} The study of the origins and historical development of medical words and terms, including their roots, prefixes, suffixes, and changes in meaning.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Medical geography} The study of how diseases, health, health-care resources, and health-related environmental factors are distributed and vary across geographic areas and populations.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Medical prefix} A prefix used in medical terminology to modify a word's meaning; examples include \textit{hyper-} (above or excessive), \textit{hypo-} (below or deficient), \textit{macro-} (large), and \textit{micro-} (small).

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Medical profession} A vocation requiring specialized medical knowledge, an ethical code, continuing learning, and a duty to place patient care and public service above self-interest.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Medical Repository} The first medical magazine and scientific journal published in the United States; founded in New York in 1797, it was published quarterly until 1824.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Medical school} An institution whose curriculum leads to a medical degree and includes foundational sciences, clinical training, patient care, and often medical research.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Medical symbol} A commonly recognized sign representing medicine, especially the Rod of Asclepius: a staff with one serpent. The winged, two-serpent caduceus is often used incorrectly as a medical symbol.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Medication} Medication is a medicine, biologic, or pharmacologic term; its use, dose, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific clinical indication.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Medifast diet} A commercial calorie-restricted weight-loss program based largely on portion-controlled meal-replacement products; its safety and suitability depend on individual health needs and professional guidance.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MEDLARS} Medical Literature Analysis and Retrieval System, an electronic bibliographic system developed by the U.S. National Library of Medicine to index and retrieve biomedical literature; it preceded MEDLINE.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MEDVAC} Abbreviation for medical evacuation: the transport of ill or injured people to a medical facility or to a location providing a higher level of care.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mega- (prefix)} A prefix derived from Greek meaning large, great, or abnormally enlarged, as in megacolon, megacardia, and megalocephaly.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Megrim} An older term for migraine, a recurrent headache disorder that may cause throbbing head pain, nausea, light or sound sensitivity, and sometimes an aura.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Melan- (prefix)} A combining form meaning dark or black, derived from the Greek \textit{melas}; it refers especially to melanin or dark pigmentation, as in melanoma and melanocyte.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Melanoderma} Increased pigmentation or darkening of the skin caused by increased melanin production or an increased number of melanocytes.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Melanoma, benign} An older or imprecise term for a benign melanocytic growth, such as a melanocytic nevus or mole; unlike malignant melanoma, it is not cancerous.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MELD} Model for End-Stage Liver Disease, a laboratory-based score used to estimate severity of advanced liver disease and help prioritize adults for liver transplantation. Traditional versions use bilirubin, creatinine, and INR; current implementations may include additional variables.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Melissophobia} An excessive or irrational fear of bees, also called apiphobia; it is a specific phobia that can cause avoidance, anxiety, or panic.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Memory span} The number of items, such as words or digits, that a person can briefly retain and recall, usually in the original order; it is a measure of short-term or working memory.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mendelism} The principles of inheritance based on Gregor Mendel's work, including segregation of alleles and independent assortment of genes; Mendelian traits may follow autosomal or X-linked dominant or recessive patterns.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Menometrorrhagia} Excessive uterine bleeding occurring both at expected menstrual times and at irregular intervals; the older term corresponds broadly to heavy and irregular menstrual bleeding and warrants evaluation for underlying causes.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Menstruation, cessation of} Cessation of menstrual periods, called amenorrhea; it is a normal part of menopause but may also result from pregnancy, illness, endocrine disorders, medications, stress, excessive exercise, or inadequate nutrition.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Met} Abbreviation for methionine, an essential sulfur-containing amino acid; it is also represented by the one-letter code M and the codons AUG.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Metabolic} Relating to metabolism, the total set of biochemical processes in a living organism, including anabolism, which builds substances, and catabolism, which breaks them down.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Methylenedioxymethamphetamine} 3,4-Methylenedioxymethamphetamine, abbreviated MDMA and commonly called ecstasy or molly, is a psychoactive drug with stimulant and hallucinogenic effects that increases serotonergic signaling and can cause serious toxicity.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MG (myasthenia gravis)} An autoimmune disorder of neuromuscular transmission that causes fluctuating, fatigable weakness, often affecting the eyes, facial muscles, swallowing, speech, or limbs; antibodies commonly target acetylcholine receptors or related proteins.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MI} Abbreviation for myocardial infarction, or heart attack: death of heart-muscle tissue caused by prolonged inadequate blood flow, usually from acute coronary artery blockage.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Miasma} A poisonous vapor or foul-smelling mist once believed to arise from decomposing matter and cause disease; the miasma theory is historically important but scientifically incorrect.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Micro-} A prefix meaning small or abnormally small, derived from the Greek \textit{mikros}; examples include microscopic, microcephaly, and microangiopathy.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Microangiopathy} Disease of small blood vessels, especially capillaries, in which vessel walls become thickened or damaged, impairing flow and allowing leakage or bleeding; diabetes is a common cause.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Microorchidism} Abnormally small testes, which may result from impaired development, genetic syndromes, or testicular atrophy after injury or infection.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Microphallus} An abnormally small penis, also called micropenis, usually defined relative to age and developmental standards; it may reflect hormonal or developmental disorders.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Microscopic anatomy} The study of cells, tissues, and fine structural details that require magnification for examination; it includes cytology and histology.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Microvascular} Relating to the microvasculature, the network of small blood vessels—generally those with an internal diameter of about 100 micrometers or less—including arterioles, capillaries, and venules.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Midget} An outdated and disparaging term historically used for a person of short stature. Preferred respectful terms include person with dwarfism or little person, according to the person's preference.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Midnight disease} An older term for hypergraphia, an unusual or excessive drive to write, sometimes associated with temporal-lobe epilepsy or other neurologic or psychiatric conditions.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Migrainous neuralgia} An older term for cluster headache, a disorder causing recurrent attacks of severe unilateral pain, usually around one eye, with ipsilateral autonomic symptoms such as tearing or nasal congestion.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Milk of Magnesia} An over-the-counter preparation of magnesium hydroxide used as an osmotic laxative for constipation and as an antacid for heartburn or indigestion; excessive or prolonged use can cause adverse effects.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Miller's lung} Hypersensitivity pneumonitis caused by inhaling material associated with the granary weevil (wheat weevil, \textit{Sitophilus granarius}), especially in people working with contaminated grain or flour.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Milligram} A metric unit of mass equal to one-thousandth of a gram; its abbreviation is mg.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Milligray} A unit of absorbed ionizing-radiation dose equal to one-thousandth of a gray, or 0.001 joule per kilogram; its abbreviation is mGy.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mimesis} Imitation or mimicry; in medicine, the term can refer to simulation of an organic disease by psychological symptoms or to one organic disease resembling another.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mind} The collection of mental processes and capacities involved in consciousness, thought, perception, memory, emotion, judgment, and behavior.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Minor} Smaller, lesser, or less significant than a related structure or condition; for example, the teres minor muscle is smaller than the teres major muscle.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mitochondrial DNA depletion syndrome MDS MDDS} A group of inherited disorders in which affected tissues contain abnormally reduced amounts of mitochondrial DNA, impairing cellular energy production. Forms may predominantly affect muscle, liver, or brain and muscle; many are autosomal recessive.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mixed-lineage leukemia} An acute leukemia in which the malignant blasts show defining features of more than one hematopoietic lineage, commonly both myeloid and lymphoid; it is also called mixed-phenotype acute leukemia.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MM} An abbreviation that may mean meningomyelocele, malignant melanoma, or multiple myeloma; in lowercase, \textit{mm} commonly means millimeter. The intended meaning depends on context.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MME} Membrane metalloendopeptidase, also called neprilysin, CD10, neutral endopeptidase, or CALLA; it is a cell-surface enzyme and marker expressed in several tissues and in some acute lymphoblastic leukemias.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mongolism} An obsolete and racist term formerly used for Down syndrome, a genetic condition usually caused by an extra copy of chromosome 21; the obsolete term should be avoided.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Monoamine oxidase inhibitor} Monoamine oxidase inhibitor is a medicine, biologic, or pharmacologic term; its use, dose, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific clinical indication.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Monoclonal} Derived from a single cell and therefore composed of cells or molecules that are genetically or immunologically identical to that original cell; monoclonal antibodies recognize one target or epitope.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Monosodium glutamate} MSG, the sodium salt of glutamic acid, used as a flavor enhancer because it contributes umami taste; it occurs naturally in foods such as tomatoes and aged cheese.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Morpheus} The Greek and Roman mythological god of dreams; the name is the source of \textit{morphine}, an opioid named for its association with sleep and dreams.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Morphology} The study or description of the form, shape, and structural features of organisms, organs, tissues, cells, or other biological structures.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mosaic} Describing a person or tissue composed of two or more genetically different cell populations derived from one zygote, often because of a post-zygotic mutation or chromosomal event; mosaicism can alter the severity and distribution of a genetic disorder.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mother} A female parent; in biology, a mother cell or structure is one from which genetically or structurally similar daughter cells or structures arise.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Motor} Relating to movement or producing movement; a motor neuron carries signals to muscle fibers to produce contraction.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MP} Abbreviation for mercaptopurine, a purine-antimetabolite drug, or for molecular pathology; the intended meaning depends on context.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MPH} Master of Public Health, a graduate degree focused on population health, disease prevention, health policy, epidemiology, and related public-health practice.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MPO} Myeloperoxidase, an enzyme in neutrophil and monocyte granules that uses hydrogen peroxide and halide ions to generate antimicrobial oxidants; MPO antibodies are also used in evaluating some autoimmune vasculitides.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MPS} Abbreviation for mucopolysaccharidosis, a group of inherited lysosomal storage disorders caused by deficient breakdown of glycosaminoglycans.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MRA} Magnetic resonance angiography, a noninvasive MRI-based technique that produces images of blood vessels and can help evaluate aneurysms, stenoses, malformations, and atherosclerosis.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MRC} Abbreviation for the Medical Reserve Corps in the United States or the Medical Research Council in the United Kingdom and some Commonwealth countries.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MRCP} Magnetic resonance cholangiopancreatography, an MRI technique that noninvasively images the bile ducts, gallbladder, and pancreatic duct without requiring endoscopic catheterization.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MRD} Minimal residual disease: a small number of malignant cells remaining after treatment, detectable by sensitive methods such as flow cytometry or PCR even when routine tests show remission.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MS/MS} Tandem mass spectrometry, an analytical technique in which ions are selected, fragmented, and analyzed by a second stage of mass spectrometry; it is used to identify and quantify molecules in clinical and research samples.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MSAFP (maternal serum alpha-fetoprotein)} A maternal blood test measuring alpha-fetoprotein during pregnancy, used as a screening component for fetal conditions such as open neural-tube defects and some chromosomal abnormalities; an abnormal result requires follow-up and is not diagnostic by itself.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MSG} Abbreviation for monosodium glutamate, the sodium salt of glutamic acid used as a flavor enhancer and contributing umami taste.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MST} Multiple subpial transection, a neurosurgical procedure in which shallow cuts are made in the cerebral cortex to interrupt seizure pathways when the epileptogenic region cannot safely be removed.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MTX} Abbreviation for methotrexate, a folate-antagonist drug used in selected cancers, autoimmune diseases, and other conditions.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mucous} Relating to mucus, the viscous fluid produced by mucous membranes and glands that lubricates surfaces and helps trap particles and microorganisms.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mucus colitis} An outdated name for irritable bowel syndrome, a disorder of gut–brain interaction characterized by recurrent abdominal pain, altered bowel habits, bloating, and sometimes mucus in the stool; it is not ulcerative colitis.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Multicentric} Having more than one center or focus; in oncology, describing separate primary tumor foci, often also called multifocal or polycentric.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Multidetector computed tomography} MDCT is CT imaging using a two-dimensional array of detector elements to acquire multiple slices simultaneously, increasing acquisition speed and enabling high-resolution applications such as CT angiography and virtual colonoscopy.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Multifactorial} Involving multiple contributing factors, such as several genes together with environmental or lifestyle factors; many common diseases and traits are multifactorial.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Multifocal} Arising from or involving two or more distinct foci or sites, such as separate tumor foci or vascular lesions.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Multipara} A person who has had two or more pregnancies that reached the stage of potential fetal viability, regardless of whether the pregnancies resulted in live births.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Multiple system failure} Failure of two or more organ systems, also called multiple-organ failure or multiple-organ dysfunction syndrome; it may occur during severe illness such as sepsis, shock, or major trauma.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Munchhausen syndrome} An obsolete name for factitious disorder imposed on self, in which a person deliberately falsifies, induces, or exaggerates illness to assume the sick role without an obvious external reward; the modern diagnostic term is preferred.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Muscle Spasm} An involuntary, often painful contraction or tightening of a muscle or muscle group, also called a muscle cramp; it may last seconds to minutes and can recur.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Muscular} Relating to muscle; the muscular system is the collection of muscles in the body, and muscular can also describe above-average muscle development.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mute} Describing a person who does not speak, whether because of a physical, neurologic, developmental, or psychological reason or by choice; current clinical language should describe the specific communication limitation or preference.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MVP} Abbreviation for mitral valve prolapse, in which one or both mitral-valve leaflets bow into the left atrium during ventricular systole; it is often mild but can sometimes cause mitral regurgitation or arrhythmias.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Myc-} A combining form meaning fungus or pertaining to fungi, derived from the Greek \textit{mykes}; examples include mycosis and mycology.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Myco-} The term Myco- is a medical word element or prefix whose precise meaning depends on the word or clinical context in which it is used.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Myeloencephalitis} Myeloencephalitis is a disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding; diagnosis and management depend on its characteristic features, causes, severity, examination, and appropriate investigations.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Myeloperoxidase} Myeloperoxidase is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{MYH} MYH is a medical abbreviation, shorthand term, or named clinical concept; its exact meaning depends on the surrounding clinical context.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Myo- (prefix)} Myo- (prefix) is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Myotonic dystrophy type 2} Myotonic dystrophy type 2 is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Myriad} Myriad is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mammary Glands (Breasts)} Paired glands of the anterior chest that produce milk after childbirth. Each breast contains lobules, ducts, connective tissue, and fat and is influenced by hormonal and reproductive changes.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
breasts; mammary glands

\medterm{Mouth (Oral Cavity)} The entry to the digestive tract, containing the teeth, tongue, and openings of salivary glands. It supports chewing, taste, swallowing, speech, and initial digestion.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
oral cavity; mouth

\medterm{Milk allergy} An immune reaction to proteins in cow's milk that may cause skin, gastrointestinal, or respiratory symptoms and, rarely, anaphylaxis.
\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mold allergy} An allergic response to mold spores that may cause nasal, eye, skin, or lower-respiratory symptoms.
\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mouth cancer} Cancer arising in the lips, gums, tongue, cheeks, floor of the mouth, palate, or other oral tissues.
\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Myocardial ischemia} Reduced blood flow and oxygen supply to heart muscle, usually from narrowed coronary arteries, potentially causing chest pain or heart damage.
\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Median arcuate ligament syndrome (MALS)} Compression of the celiac artery and nearby nerves by the median arcuate ligament, potentially causing post-meal abdominal pain and weight loss.
\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Multisystem inflammatory syndrome in children (MIS-C)} A serious inflammatory condition occurring after SARS-CoV-2 infection, affecting multiple organs and sometimes causing shock or cardiac dysfunction.
\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Mandible (Lower Jaw)}The large movable bone forming the lower jaw and carrying the lower teeth.



\textbf{Synonyms}:
Lower Jaw


\medterm{Maxilla (Upper Jaw)}The paired bones forming the upper jaw, the front part of the hard palate, and parts of the walls of the nose and eye sockets.



\textbf{Synonyms}:
Upper Jaw
